class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_abstract\" id=\"abstract\">\n<h6 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_abstract\">Abstract</h6><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"id1.1\">We propose a novel composite reward function for a reinforcement learning (RL) trading agent that explicitly balances return and risk by combining four differentiable components—annualized return, downside risk, differential return, and the Treynor ratio. Unlike traditional single-metric objectives (e.g., Sharpe or cumulative return), which can encourage reward hacking or over-optimization of one aspect of trading, our formulation is inherently modular and weighted <math alttext=\"w_{1}\\ldots w_{4}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"id1.1.m1.1a\"><mrow id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><msub id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\"><mi id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.2\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.2.cmml\">w</mi><mn id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.3\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.3.cmml\">1</mn></msub><mo id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\">⁢</mo><mi id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathvariant=\"normal\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">…</mi><mo id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1a\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\">⁢</mo><msub id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.cmml\"><mi id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.2\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.2.cmml\">w</mi><mn id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.3\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.3.cmml\">4</mn></msub></mrow><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1\"><times id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.1\"></times><apply id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.1.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.2.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.2\">𝑤</ci><cn id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.3.cmml\" type=\"integer\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.2.3\">1</cn></apply><ci id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.3\">…</ci><apply id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.1.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.2.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.2\">𝑤</ci><cn id=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.3.cmml\" type=\"integer\" xref=\"id1.1.m1.1.1.4.3\">4</cn></apply></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1c\">w_{1}\\ldots w_{4}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"id1.1.m1.1d\">italic_w start_POSTSUBSCRIPT 1 end_POSTSUBSCRIPT … italic_w start_POSTSUBSCRIPT 4 end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math> to allow practitioners to encode diverse investor preferences and adjust emphasis across multiple financial goals. We also demonstrate hyperparameter tuning of these weights via grid search to identify configurations that align with different risk–return profiles. We present the full mathematical form of the reward, derive closed-form gradients for each term to ensure compatibility with gradient-based learning, and analyze key theoretical properties including monotonicity, boundedness, and modularity. This abstract framework serves as a general blueprint for constructing robust, multi-objective reward functions in complex trading environments and can be readily extended with additional risk measures or adaptive weighting schemes.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S1\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">1 </span>Introduction</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p1.1\">In financial trading and portfolio optimization, designing effective objective functions that capture both return and risk is essential. Modern Portfolio Theory (MPT) emphasizes this trade-off <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib3\" title=\"\">3</a>]</cite>, and performance metrics such as the Sharpe ratio <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib7\" title=\"\">7</a>]</cite> and the Treynor ratio <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib11\" title=\"\">11</a>]</cite> extend this notion by measuring returns relative to total and systematic risk, respectively. In reinforcement learning (RL) applications to trading, prior work has shown that directly optimizing such financial performance metrics can be effective <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib5\" title=\"\">5</a>]</cite>. However, traditional RL reward formulations often rely on a single metric—such as cumulative return or Sharpe ratio—which can lead to reward hacking and poor generalization in the inherently multi-objective landscape of financial markets.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p2.1\">To address this limitation, we propose a modular, multi-objective reward framework that explicitly balances return and risk using four domain-informed components. This formulation enables the reward to reflect a broader range of investor preferences and reduces the risk of over-optimization on any single aspect of trading performance.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p3.1\">Our main contributions are as follows:</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<ul class=\"ltx_itemize\" id=\"S1.I1\">\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i1\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1\">We introduce a composite reward function <math alttext=\"R\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">R</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑅</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1c\">R</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S1.I1.i1.p1.1.m1.1d\">italic_R</annotation></semantics></math> that combines annualized return, downside risk (penalty), benchmark outperformance, and the Treynor ratio, and provide its full mathematical formulation.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i2\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1\">We offer comprehensive financial interpretations of each component in <math alttext=\"R\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">R</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑅</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1c\">R</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S1.I1.i2.p1.1.m1.1d\">italic_R</annotation></semantics></math>—including return, risk penalty, alpha, and systematic risk adjustment—supported by key references to financial theory <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib6\" title=\"\">6</a>, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib9\" title=\"\">9</a>]</cite>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i3\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i3.p1.1\">We derive and analyze the partial derivatives (gradients) of each component of <math alttext=\"R\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S1.I1.i3.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S1.I1.i3.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S1.I1.i3.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S1.I1.i3.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">R</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" confirming that the reward is piecewise differentiable and thus suitable for gradient-based optimization.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i4\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i4.p1.1\">We propose a grid-search procedure for tuning the weight coefficients <math alttext=\"w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" end_POSTSUBSCRIPT , italic_w start_POSTSUBSCRIPT 4 end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>. We explore how variations in these weights influence the agent’s risk preferences and decision-making.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i5\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i5.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i5.p1.1\">We investigate the theoretical properties of the reward, including monotonicity (the relationship between returns and risk), boundedness of each term, and modularity (the independence of components), ensuring a robust design.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S1.I1.i6\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.I1.i6.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.I1.i6.p1.1\">We highlight the practical implications of our reward framework for RL-based stock trading and portfolio management, demonstrating how the agent’s objectives align with diverse financial goals.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n</ul>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S2\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">2 </span>Related Work</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.p1.1\">Modern portfolio theory, initiated by Markowitz (1952) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib3\" title=\"\">3</a>]</cite>, formalized the trade-off between expected return and variance in portfolio selection. Later, the Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM) and related metrics were developed to evaluate portfolio performance relative to market risk <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib6\" title=\"\">6</a>]</cite>. The Sharpe ratio uses total volatility (standard deviation) as a risk measure <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib7\" title=\"\">7</a>]</cite>, while the Treynor ratio uses systematic risk (beta) relative to a benchmark <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib11\" title=\"\">11</a>]</cite>. Both are foundational in risk-adjusted performance evaluation. The concept of downside risk (e.g., semi-variance or Sortino ratio) emphasizes losses below a threshold (such as zero or a minimum return) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib9\" title=\"\">9</a>]</cite>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.p2.1\">In reinforcement learning for finance, Moody and Saffell <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib5\" title=\"\">5</a>]</cite> pioneered the idea of optimizing trading policies by maximizing financial performance functions directly, such as profits, Sharpe ratio, or other utility functions. Subsequent work in deep RL for portfolio management has used similar reward structures. However, most existing RL studies optimize a single metric (e.g., Sharpe or cumulative return). Our approach generalizes this by linearly combining multiple performance criteria into one reward. This multi-component reward captures a richer set of financial objectives: maximizing returns, limiting downside risk, outperforming a benchmark (alpha), and achieving high risk-adjusted returns per unit of systematic risk.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S3\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">3 </span>Methodology</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.p1.6\">We consider a portfolio of <math alttext=\"N\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">N</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑁</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1c\">N</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.p1.1.m1.1d\">italic_N</annotation></semantics></math> assets. id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.4.m4.5.5.3.3.1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.4.m4.5.5.3.3.1.1.1.2\">𝑅</ci><ci id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.4.m4.5.5.3.3.1.1.1.3.cmml\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.4.m4.5c\">\\beta_{p}=\\operatorname{Cov}(R_{p},R_{m})/\\operatorname{Var}(R_{m})</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.4.m4.5d\">italic_β start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_p end_POSTSUBSCRIPT = roman_Cov ( italic_R start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_p end_POSTSUBSCRIPT , italic_R start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_m end_POSTSUBSCRIPT ) / roman_Var ( italic_R start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_m end_POSTSUBSCRIPT )</annotation></semantics></math>, capturing the agent’s sensitivity to market fluctuations.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.1\">This risk-adjusted measure, termed the <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.1\">differential return (DR)</em>, represents the average outperformance of a portfolio relative to a benchmark, normalized by systematic (market) risk. It reflects a reward-per-unit-risk framework similar in spirit to the alpha from CAPM <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib6\" title=\"\">6</a>]</cite>, but explicitly accounts for the portfolio’s market exposure.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1\">Our formulation is a <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1.1\">simplified differential return (SDR)</em> derived from the foundational work by Simpson and formalized by Alam <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">1</a>]</cite>. While Alam proposes more complex versions incorporating risk ratios and Sharpe-based scaling, our SDR focuses on <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1.2\">systematic risk adjustment via beta only</em>, avoiding explicit benchmark beta terms or volatility scaling. This maintains the key intention — rewarding portfolios that consistently outperform the benchmark <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1.3\">on a risk-adjusted basis</em> — while reducing sensitivity to unstable second-order statistics (like benchmark variance or cross-volatility) and non-convex functions that may hinder stable optimization in reinforcement learning.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.1\">We later show the correctness of our formulation in our theoretical analysis (Section&nbsp;<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#S4\" title=\"4 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Risk-Aware Reinforcement Learning Reward for Financial Trading\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">4</span></a>). The concept of differential return itself originates from Simpson’s work <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib8\" title=\"\">8</a>]</cite>, with subsequent refinements by Alam <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">1</a>]</cite>, whose formulation informed our choice of SDR as a practical and principled performance metric.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS0.SSS0.Px4\">\n<h5 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Treynor Ratio</h5><button class=\"sr-only button\" id=\"S3.SS2.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\mathcal{R}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS2.p1.1.m1.1d\">caligraphic_R</annotation></semantics></math> has a clear economic meaning:</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<ul class=\"ltx_itemize\" id=\"S3.I1\">\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S3.I1.i1\" xref=\"S3.I1.i1.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S3.I1.i1.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i1.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S3.I1.i1.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>):</span> This measures the overall growth of the portfolio over the evaluation period <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib3\" title=\"\">3</a>]</cite>. Maximizing this term alone corresponds to pure return maximization, aligning with the classical ”maximize return” objective in modern portfolio theory.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S3.I1.i2\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1\">Downside Risk (<math alttext=\"D_{\\mathrm{down}}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1a\"><msub id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mi id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\">D</mi><mi id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">down</mi></msub><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2\">𝐷</ci><ci id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3\">down</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1c\">D_{\\mathrm{down}}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.1.1.m1.1d\">italic_D start_POSTSUBSCRIPT roman_down end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>):</span> This penalizes negative returns <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib9\" title=\"\">9</a>]</cite>. It is conceptually aligned with the Sortino ratio, which focuses on downside deviation rather than total variance. A larger <math alttext=\"D_{\\mathrm{down}}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1a\"><msub id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mi id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.2.cmml\">D</mi><mi id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.3\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">down</mi></msub><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.2\">𝐷</ci><ci id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1.1.3\">down</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1c\">D_{\\mathrm{down}}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.2.m1.1d\">italic_D start_POSTSUBSCRIPT roman_down end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math> indicates greater drawdowns, which reduces the reward <math alttext=\"\\mathcal{R}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1a\"><mi class=\"ltx_font_mathcaligraphic\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1.1.cmml\">ℛ</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1.1\">ℛ</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1c\">\\mathcal{R}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.I1.i2.p1.3.m2.1d\">caligraphic_R</annotation></semantics></math>, thereby incentivizing capital preservation.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S3.I1.i3\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.I1.i3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.I1.i3.p1.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.I1.i3.p1.1.1\">Differential Return (DR):</span> We adopt a simplified differential return (SDR) formulation tailored to reinforcement learning agents. This approach is inspired by Alam’s formalization <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">1</a>]</cite>, building on the original concept introduced by Simpson <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib8\" title=\"\">8</a>]</cite>. The underlying idea of risk-adjusted benchmark outperformance is rooted in the Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib6\" title=\"\">6</a>]</cite>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_item\" id=\"S3.I1.i4\" style=\"list-style-type:none;\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_item\">•</span>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1\">Treynor Ratio (<math alttext=\"T_{\\mathrm{ry}}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1a\"><msub id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mi id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\">T</mi><mi id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">ry</mi></msub><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.2\">𝑇</ci><ci id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1.1.3\">ry</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1c\">T_{\\mathrm{ry}}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.I1.i4.p1.1.1.m1.1d\">italic_T start_POSTSUBSCRIPT roman_ry end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>):</span> This term captures return per unit of systematic risk <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib11\" title=\"\">11</a>]</cite>. It rewards agents that either reduce beta exposure or achieve high returns relative to beta. Alam <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">1</a>]</cite> emphasizes the Treynor ratio as a core element of risk-adjusted performance and a foundational component in differential return models.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</li>\n</ul>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS2.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS2.p2.1\">These components together create a rich reward signal. For example, an allocation might achieve high raw return but suffer from high risk; the downside and Treynor terms will moderate its reward. Conversely, a low-risk strategy might get a good Treynor score even if raw return is modest. The benchmark term ensures attention to market-relative performance, avoiding trivial solutions that merely follow the index.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S3.SS3\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">3.3 id=\"S3.SS3.p1.2\">Tuning the weights <math alttext=\"\\{w_{i}\\}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mrow id=\"S3.SS3.p1.1.m1.1.1.1\" }</annotation></semantics></math> is treated as a hyperparameter-optimization task on the reward function itself. In our experiments, we trained multiple agents—each with a different weight configuration sampled via a simple grid search over the simplex (<math alttext=\"\\sum_{i}w_{i}=1\" class=\"ltx_Math\" end_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_w start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_i end_POSTSUBSCRIPT = 1</annotation></semantics></math>)—and evaluated their risk–return performance on historical data. This process clearly demonstrates how emphasizing downside-risk penalties or benchmark outperformance shifts the agent toward more conservative or aggressive trading behaviors.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.p2.1\">While our study uses grid search as a proof of concept, any hyperparameter-tuning framework (e.g., Bayesian optimization or population-based training) can be applied in the same way: by treating <math alttext=\"\\{w_{i}\\}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.p2.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.p2.1.m1.1a\"><mrow id=\"S3.SS3.p2.1.m1.1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.p2.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\"><mo end_POSTSUBSCRIPT }</annotation></semantics></math> as tunable parameters and retraining the model under each candidate set. This approach allows practitioners to systematically customize the composite reward to match different market regimes and investor preferences without modifying the reward’s core design.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S4\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">4 </span>Theoretical Analysis</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.p1.1\">We analyze the composite reward function <math alttext=\"R\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">R</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑅</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1c\">R</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.p1.1.m1.1d\">italic_R</annotation></semantics></math> and its components, focusing on differentiability, gradients, and functional characteristics. We show that this reward function is theoretically sound for reinforcement learning in financial trading, satisfying desirable properties such as monotonicity, differentiability, boundedness, and convergence under standard optimization procedures.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S4.SS1\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">4.1 </span>Monotonicity and Differentiability</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3\">Each term in <math alttext=\"\\mathcal{R}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi class=\"ltx_font_mathcaligraphic\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">ℛ</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1\">ℛ</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\mathcal{R}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1d\">caligraphic_R</annotation></semantics></math> is monotonic in its respective financial variable, and can be independently treated as a convex optimization problem for the financial terms to improve risk-adjusted returns. To show this, we consider the gradients of individual terms of <math alttext=\"\\mathcal{R}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1a\"><mi class=\"ltx_font_mathcaligraphic\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">ℛ</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1.1\">ℛ</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1c\">\\mathcal{R}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.2.m2.1d\">caligraphic_R</annotation></semantics></math> with respect to financial terms. We also show that <math alttext=\"R\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1a\"><mi id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\">R</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1b\"><ci id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1.1\">𝑅</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1c\">R</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.3.m3.1d\">italic_R</annotation></semantics></math> is differentiable with respect to all weights, making it suitable for backpropagation-based reinforcement learning algorithms. For simplicity, we assume weights are rebalanced only at time 1 and held constant (the analysis extends to dynamic rebalancing with time-indexed weights).</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1\">\n<h5 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Annualized encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.4.m4.1d\">italic_β &gt; 0</annotation></semantics></math> is the portfolio’s systematic risk. This Simplified Differential Return (SDR) term captures the differential return originally formulated by Simpson <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib8\" title=\"\">8</a>]</cite> and refined by Alam <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite\">[<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">1</a>]</cite>. Drawing insights from Alam’s approach, we define the metric as the difference between the portfolio and its benchmark, normalized by market exposure.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" (AVGO) stock, as shown in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#S6.F6\" title=\"Figure 6 ‣ 6.1 Exceptional Returns on Broadcom (AVGO) ‣ 6 Performance Analysis and Benchmark Comparisons ‣ Risk-Aware Reinforcement Learning Reward for Financial Trading\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">6</span></a>:</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure\" id=\"S6.F6\"><img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"324\" id=\"S6.F6.g1\" src=\"extracted/6513204/broadcom-avgo.png\" width=\"598\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\">Figure 6: </span>Our model’s performance on Broadcom (AVGO) stock, achieving a 36.4% annualized return with a Sharpe ratio of 1.042.</figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS1.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS1.p2.1\">The model achieved an annualized return of 36.4% on Broadcom Inc. (AVGO), significantly outperforming the S&amp;P 500’s historical average of approximately 10-12%. With a Sharpe ratio of 1.042, the model maintains a reasonable risk-adjusted performance, suggesting higher returns than the market at a controlled risk level.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS1.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS1.p3.1\">This performance is particularly noteworthy when compared to elite quantitative hedge funds. For instance, Renaissance Technologies, widely regarded as one of the most successful quantitative investment firms, achieves approximately 40% annualized returns. Our model’s performance is competitive with such top-tier quantitative strategies, despite using a more transparent and interpretable approach.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S6.SS2\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">6.2 </span>Market Downturn Resilience: Netflix Case Study</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS2.p1.1\">A critical test of any trading strategy is its performance during market downturns. id=\"S6.SS2.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1\">This resilience during market downturns highlights one of the key advantages of our composite reward function: by explicitly accounting for downside risk through the <math alttext=\"\\sigma_{\\mathrm{down}}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1a\"><msub id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mi id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\">σ</mi><mi id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.3\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">down</mi></msub><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.2\">𝜎</ci><ci id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1.1.3\">down</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1c\">\\sigma_{\\mathrm{down}}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S6.SS2.p3.1.m1.1d\">italic_σ start_POSTSUBSCRIPT roman_down end_POSTSUBSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math> component and incorporating benchmark-relative performance, the agent learns to effectively manage risk during adverse market conditions while still capitalizing on available opportunities.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S6.SS3\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">6.3 </span>Generalization Across Multiple Stocks</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS3.p1.1\">The agent’s true strength lies in its ability to generalize across heterogeneous assets. By training simultaneously on AAPL, GOOGL, MSFT, NFLX and TSLA, the policy learns to identify and exploit overarching market signals—such as momentum shifts, volatility clustering, and cross-asset correlations—rather than memorizing a single price series.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure\" id=\"S6.F10\"><img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"268\" id=\"S6.F10.g1\" src=\"extracted/6513204/closing_prices_of_stocks.png\" width=\"509\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\">Figure 10: </span>Daily closing prices of the five major tech stocks (AAPL, GOOGL, MSFT, NFLX, TSLA) used for training our multi-stock trading agent. The diverse price patterns and volatility profiles across these assets provide a challenging environment for generalization.</figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS3.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS3.p2.1\">Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#S6.F10\" title=\"Figure 10 ‣ 6.3 Generalization Across Multiple Stocks ‣ 6 Performance Analysis and Benchmark Comparisons ‣ Risk-Aware Reinforcement Learning Reward for Financial Trading\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">10</span></a> illustrates the closing prices of the five major tech stocks used in our multi-stock trading experiment. The significant variations in price levels, volatility, and correlation structures among these assets create a complex trading environment that requires the agent to develop sophisticated decision-making capabilities beyond single-asset strategies.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS3.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS3.p3.1\">When deployed on this diverse asset pool, our RL agent demonstrated remarkable adaptability. During bull markets (e.g., 2017–2021), the agent progressively increased allocations to high-beta names (NFLX, TSLA) as sustained uptrends and relative strength emerged. Conversely, in the 2022 technology pullback, it rotated into defensive large-cap names (MSFT, AAPL), demonstrating an implicit understanding of each stock’s risk–return profile. This dynamic reallocation is feasible because the composite reward continues to evaluate performance via Sortino, Treynor and differential return in real time.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage\"></div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure\" id=\"S6.F11\"><img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"252\" id=\"S6.F11.g1\" src=\"extracted/6513204/our_bot_performance.jpeg\" width=\"509\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\">Figure 11: </span>Performance of our multi-stock trading agent across the five-stock portfolio. The agent achieves consistent positive returns while effectively managing drawdowns during market volatility periods, demonstrating the effectiveness of our composite reward function in multi-asset environments.</figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS3.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS3.p4.1\">As shown in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04358v1#S6.F11\" title=\"Figure 11 ‣ 6.3 Generalization Across Multiple Stocks ‣ 6 Performance Analysis and Benchmark Comparisons ‣ Risk-Aware Reinforcement Learning Reward for Financial Trading\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">11</span></a>, the multi-stock portfolio managed by our agent achieved impressive risk-adjusted returns. The performance chart reveals several key strengths of our approach when trading multiple stocks simultaneously.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.SS3.p5\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.SS3.p5.1\">This multi-asset performance confirms that our risk-aware reward formulation can effectively scale to more complex portfolio management tasks while maintaining strong risk-adjusted returns. The agent’s success across heterogeneous assets with different fundamental characteristics validates the generalizability of our approach beyond single-stock trading scenarios.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S7\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">7 </span>Discussion</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S7.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S7.p1.1\">Our composite reward function integrates multiple financial metrics into a unified objective, enabling an agent to pursue balanced performance and risk management simultaneously. By combining return maximization with explicit downside protection and benchmark-relative components, the reward shape guides the agent toward smoother equity growth while capturing market upside.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S7.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S7.p2.1\">The experimental results confirm that our risk-aware RL trading agent consistently outperforms traditional algorithmic strategies across various market regimes. In volatile environments, it achieves higher peak returns with notably lower drawdowns; in calmer markets, it delivers steadier gains without sacrificing upside potential.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S7.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S7.p3.1\">Looking ahead, we plan to explore alternative reward parameterizations, such as incorporating a differential Sharpe ratio component to more directly optimize for risk-adjusted performance. By dynamically adjusting reward terms based on rolling Sharpe calculations or tail-risk measures, the agent could emphasize strategies that maximize risk-adjusted returns under varying market conditions. Investigating the impact of these enhanced parameters in live simulation environments will be a key direction for future research.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_bibliography\" id=\"bib\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_bibliography\">References</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<ul class=\"ltx_biblist\">\n<li class=\"ltx_bibitem\" id=\"bib.bib1\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_role_refnum ltx_tag_bibitem\">Alam [2021]<button class=\"back-to-reference-btn\" aria-label=\"Back to the article\">↑</button></span>\n<span class=\"ltx_bibblock\">\nS.M.&nbsp;Ikhtiar Alam.\n\n</span>\n<span class=\"ltx_bibblock\">Portfolio performance and risk penalty measurement with differential return.\n\n</span>\n<span class=\"ltx_bibblock\"><em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"bib.bib1.1.1\">International Journal of Services Sciences</em>, 2:24–32, 2021.\n\n</span>\n<span class=\"ltx_bibblock\">URL <a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter\" href=\"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/356127405_Portfolio_Performance_and_Risk_Penalty_Measurement_with_Differential_Return\" title=\"\">https://www.researchgate.net/publication/356127405_Portfolio_Performance_and_Risk_Penalty_Measurement_with_Differential_Return</a>.\n\n</span>\n</li>\n<li class=\"ltx_bibitem\" id=\"bib.bib2\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_role_refnum ltx_tag_bibitem\">Borkar [2009]<button class=\"back-to-reference-btn\" aria-label=\"Back to the article\">↑</button></span>\n<span class=\"ltx_bibblock\">\nVivek&nbsp;S.
